## 2026-09-18T02:05:00+00:00 - deep-review
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [the-binding-problem](/topics/the-binding-problem/)
- **Word count**: 4089 → 3999 (-90; was `hard_warning`, now `soft_warning`. NB `length.py` gates on `>= hard`, so 4000 itself trips — 3999 is the real target)
- **Critical issues addressed**: 1
- **Medium issues addressed**: 2
- **Enhancements made**: 1
- **Verdict**: NOT a no-op. The 07-18 stability note said the article was converged and the §2.4 citation pass discharged. Treated as a marker of which lenses were unrun rather than certification — correctly, because the citation pass was the lens that had never been run on the article's actual text. Root cause: the 06-26 and 07-18 ledgers both recorded the reference as "Revonsuo **2006**" (*Inner Presence*), which is not in this article's References; the article cites Revonsuo **1999**. Each ledger then justified skipping the re-check against the previous ledger rather than against the article.
- **Critical — source/Map conflation at the foundational paragraph.** §The Foundational Distinction glossed BP2 as "why coordinated processing produces unified experience … the hard problem applied to unity" directly under "(Revonsuo 1999)". Read the full Revonsuo 1999 PDF: his BP2 is a *how*-question about mechanisms ("How do the brain mechanisms actually construct the phenomenal object?"), glossed as "finding the neural mechanisms which map the unified contents in phenomenal consciousness to corresponding neural entites in the brain". Grep returns ZERO occurrences of "hard problem", "explanatory gap" or "Chalmers"; he is a biological naturalist who expects it to yield. The article had cited its source as authority for an escalation the source rejects — and this is the article's load-bearing move, since the "BP2 gap" refrain recurs in all five varieties. Fixed: BP2 now carries Revonsuo's wording and intent, names his naturalism, and marks the escalation as the Map's own.
- **Medium — Bayne & Chalmers over-read.** "Conjoint phenomenology" was said to give character "exceeding the sum" of the parts. Their text defines it by *subsumption* ("a phenomenology of having both states at once that subsumes the phenomenology of the individual states"); "exceed" occurs 0 times against 74 for "subsume". Realigned to subsumption with the excess claim marked as the Map's — matching the companion `concepts/binding-problem`, which already had it right. The "extends from perception to thought" half verified CORRECT (B&C explicitly include "perceptual, bodily, emotional, and cognitive experiences").
- **Medium — orphan references.** Prior reviews certified "no orphans"; measured, only 2 of 9 entries had an inline anchor. Added anchors for Baars et al. 2013, Stein and Stanford 2008, Mudrik et al. 2014, James 1890, Jerath and Beveridge 2019, plus years on Nagel and Bayne & Chalmers. All 10 entries now anchored.
- **Enhancement — Smythies provenance.** Revonsuo does not claim BP1/BP2: he quotes Smythies coining the labels (1994b p.321) and says "there is only one author in the literature, namely, John R. Smythies, who clearly distinguishes these two types of binding". "Smythies" occurred in ZERO files corpus-wide while the distinction is used in nine live articles. Added to text and References. The provenance is better than what it replaces: coined in a paper titled *Requiem for the Identity Theory*, popularised by a biological naturalist.
- **Checked, no defect (recorded to prevent re-litigation)**: the "~100ms" global-workspace figure. I suspected it against Dehaene's ~270-300ms ignition number — wrong suspicion, it is cited to Baars, not Dehaene, and the Baars et al. 2013 full text says "spread globally to all other regions for ∼100 ms" and "the ∼100 ms conscious integration time of different sensory inputs". No change. Also: the Nagel passage's missing verbatim quote is not a regression — commit 7385ba8b35 correctly replaced it with a paraphrase that restores the dropped "specially contrived laboratory situations" qualifier.
- **Per-cite ledger**: all 10 references verified at publisher of record, primary text read for Revonsuo 1999, Bayne & Chalmers 2003 and Baars et al. 2013. Revonsuo 1999 real-correct (DOI 10.1006/ccog.1999.0384); Smythies 1994 real-correct (Inquiry 37(3), 311-329); Treisman & Gelade 1980, Nagel 1971, Baars et al. 2013, Mudrik et al. 2014, Stein & Stanford 2008, Jerath & Beveridge 2019, James 1890 all real-correct. - **Verdict: CONFIRMED, and fixed.** This was an assess-first task where a decline was a legitimate outcome; the decline criterion in the task (a claim that a minimal or narrative self suffices for *responsibility*) does not describe the passage, which is about what survives contemplative deconstruction, so the criterion had to be replaced by the discriminator.
- **The discriminator applied explicitly** ([where-the-substance-commitment-enters](/concepts/where-the-substance-commitment-enters/) §The Diagnostic — *does the argument treat the agent as an ontological source, or does it only need consciousness to be irreducible?*). The passage is **mixed, and that is the defect**:
  - Its *local* content sits on the bare-dualism row — the witness persists, stripped to "irreducible thisness". A haecceity/individuation claim needs no persisting substance-agent, so read in isolation the sentence is a false positive.
  - Its *term* does not. "The substantial self" is defined by this article on the substance-leaning row, twice and explicitly: L107 (§The Causal Argument) *"Only a substantial self—a genuine [causal agent](/concepts/agent-causation/)—can do these things"*; L113 *"this conscious being with this haecceity, **making these choices**"*; and again at L142 (§Relation to Site Perspective) *"there must be an entity doing the influencing… a substantial particular whose choices are real."* That is the authorship row.

They target different layers."* asserted anattā-compatibility for a term the article had already loaded with agent causation, three paragraphs earlier, in the same argumentative chain and with no re-scoping. This is the **silent-inheritance** failure mode the Diagnostic names. The compatibility holds for the thin-subject/thisness component and fails for the agent-causal component; the bundled term hid the split.
- **Why the existing mitigation does not discharge it** — L119 concedes the deeper Madhyamaka objection (*śūnyatā* applied to witness-awareness). But that concession is about the *findability* of the witness, i.e. the individuation question. The **agent-causal** divergence was marked nowhere in the article, so the mitigation the triage noted ("mitigated but never withdrawn") does not reach the half that was actually defective.
- **The fix is re-framing, not deletion.** The article's filter — that anattā targets the constructed self while something survives — is preserved; the compatibility claim is scoped. Installed the canonical [buddhism-and-dualism](/concepts/buddhism-and-dualism/) L94 formulation verbatim in structure, as in all four sibling fixes: *"What the traditions share is mental causation… What they do not share is [agent causation](/concepts/agent-causation/), which routes that causation through the persisting substance-bearing subject anattā denies"*, plus the pointed clause *"and that subject is exactly what the causal argument above requires"*, the discriminator sentence, and the bedrock marker citing **[P-I1](/positions/individuation-and-subjecthood/#p-i1)** (bare id, as in the 93bfa6bb sibling, not a piped register wikilink). - **The defect**: `concepts/agent-causation` defines agents as persisting substances exercising causal powers, and [tenets](/tenets/) requires "a persisting subject that exercises causal powers in its own right". *Anattā* denies exactly that subject, so a passage claiming the Map's selection framework accommodates no-self asserts what no-self denies.
- **The three quantum-coherence files carried near-identical prose, checked individually rather than assumed byte-identical** — `quantum-coherence-and-binding-evidence` lacks the "If consciousness involves quantum selection, what selects?" sentence the other two carry, so the replacements differ by one clause. All three closed their *Contemplative Evidence* section with: *"Process philosophy's response through process haecceity—the particular actual occasion as selective locus—accommodates both momentary selection and Buddhist phenomenology without requiring a substantial self."* That is the same each-moment-selecting move as loci (b) and (d): a selective locus individuated moment by moment is not the persisting subject the agent-causal reading needs.
- **The fix — re-framing, not deletion**: the canonical formulation at `concepts/buddhism-and-dualism` L94 was reused, in the compact form the sibling archive fix `archive/concepts/luck-objection` L143 already carries, so the archive tree does not drift from the live corpus. Each passage now (i) concedes that *anattā* presses and that the actual occasion does not absorb the pressure, (ii) grants process haecceity its real work — *this* occasion is distinct from another without permanence — while denying that individuating a selective locus constitutes a subject whose selection an outcome is, (iii) names *mental causation* as the shared claim that [Bidirectional Interaction](/tenets/#bidirectional-interaction) asserts and [agent causation](/concepts/agent-causation/) as the unshared one, and (iv) marks the residue as bedrock, citing `P-I1`.
- **The discriminator was needed here and is now cited.** These are articles whose whole subject is quantum selection, so a bare bedrock note would read as conceding the article. [where-the-substance-commitment-enters](/concepts/where-the-substance-commitment-enters/) §The Diagnostic tabulates *"Mental states cause physical effects at quantum indeterminacies"* → **no substance required** against the authorship/desert row → **substance-leaning**. Each passage now carries that split in one sentence: the bare claim that consciousness biases quantum outcomes needs no persisting subject; the question of *who* selects does. That is what lets the articles concede everything *anattā* wins without conceding their own mechanism.

- **The defect**: *"Buddhism's *anattā* might seem to undermine agent causation, but Buddhism does not deny that consciousness exists—it denies permanent ownership. Selection can occur moment to moment without an eternal selector; process [haecceity](/concepts/haecceity/), being *this* selecting awareness at each moment, suffices for authorship."* `concepts/agent-causation` and [tenets](/tenets/) (Tenet 1 practical-ontology note) both require a persisting subject exercising causal powers in its own right; *anattā* denies exactly that, so the paragraph claimed to absorb an objection it cannot absorb.
- **The driver's question answered — process haecceity *does* do real work, and the fix is scoped rather than flattening.** The task asked whether the process-haecceity machinery genuinely supplies a non-substantial authorship the Map endorses elsewhere. **It supplies half of it.** [haecceity](/concepts/haecceity/) §Process Haecceitism (its own coinage, L113) makes four commitments — (1) an experiential process is individuated by its causal history; (2) two processes can share all qualitative properties while remaining numerically distinct; (3) the process, not a substrate, bears the haecceity; (4) there is a fact about which process is mine — and L177 states the Map's commitment as neutral between bearers: *"whether that fact attaches to an enduring substance or an impermanent experiential process."* **Every one of those four is an *individuation* claim. None is a causal-powers claim.** So process haecceity genuinely delivers *which* stream a selection belongs to, without permanence — and the fix preserves that, rather than deleting it. What it does not deliver is a subject that *exercises* the power, which is what the word "authorship" was doing in the defective sentence.

## 2026-09-17T20:27:22+00:00 - deep-review
- **Status**: Success
- **File**: [phenomenology-of-linguistic-failure](/topics/phenomenology-of-linguistic-failure/)
- **Word count**: 2052 → 2189 (+137), soft threshold 3000 — no length pressure
- **Critical issues addressed**: 3
- **Medium issues addressed**: 2
- **Enhancements made**: 1 (argument strengthened, see below)
- **Verdict — NOT the predicted no-op.** The 2026-06-25 and 2026-07-17 reviews both declared this article converged and predicted "a genuine no-op unless the body changes". The body had not changed (video embed 2026-08-16; one Further Reading line from apex-evolve). Three defects were live anyway. The prior passes ran citation **metadata** and treated a clean metadata ledger as discharging §2.4; metadata was never the exposure.
- **Critical 1 — quote fidelity.** `"not *how* the world is, but *that* it is."` was carried in quotation marks with an unmarked internal elision. Genuine Ogden/Ramsey 1922 *Tractatus* 6.44: "Not *how* the world is, is the mystical, but *that* it is." Verbatim text restored, 6.44 locator added. The 2026-06-25 pass had certified it as "a faithful condensation" — which certifies the reading, not the verbatim status the quotation marks assert.
- ⚠️ **Sourcing trap recorded: Project Gutenberg #5740 is catalogued as the Ogden translation and is not.** Its 6.44 is Pears & McGuinness ("It is not how things are in the world that is mystical, but that it exists"); its prop 7 is "What we cannot speak about we must pass over in silence" and `grep -ciF whereof` over the whole file returns **0** (Ogden: "Whereof one cannot speak, thereof one must be silent") — yet its 6.54 *is* Ogden. It is a mixed text. Verification redone against the Ludwig Wittgenstein Project transcription of the 1922 Kegan Paul edition, using prop 7 as the translation-discriminating control. My first "control" (the 6.54 ladder) validated the file and would have licensed the wrong wording; it was prop 7 that caught it.
- **Critical 2 — orphan reference.** Chalmers (1996) sat in References with `grep -nF Chalmers` returning exactly one hit: the References line itself. Never cited inline, through five reviews that each recorded it "real-correct" on metadata. §2.4 step 5 makes this critical. The natural host was already in the body, attributed to nobody — now "as Chalmers's (1996) zombie argument dramatises".
- **Critical 3 — strawman regress.** The article argued a functional-computational view's evaluative stance "would require a further processing layer, which would itself need monitoring, generating a genuine regress". That premise is exactly what higher-order theories deny by design: the monitoring state need not itself be monitored because it need not itself be conscious. Rewritten to state the reply, concede it, and relocate the weight onto what the reply does not answer — why a mismatch-registering mechanism should feel like anything, arriving as *correct-but-betraying* rather than as a silent error signal. Net strengthening, not a retreat. Engagement is with a generic position, not a named opponent, so §2.6 classification is not strictly triggered; shape of the repair is Mode Two (identify the unearned move) closing into honest question-marking rather than claimed refutation.
- **Medium 4 — cited-author-stance leg (§2.4 step 8), never previously run.** Nagel and Levine were both recruited for a dualist divide; neither draws it and one disclaims it. Verified verbatim against raw sources, not summarisers: Nagel (1974) "It would be a mistake to conclude that physicalism must be false"; Levine (1983) "One cannot conclude from my version of the argument that materialism is false", his stated purpose being "to transform Kripke's argument from a metaphysical one into an epistemological one". Neither was misattributed, so no metadata pass could have caught it. Both stances now marked in the body — the convergence the article claims is a shared *observation* between non-dualists and the Map, which is a better datum than a manufactured shared conclusion.
- **Medium 5** — `[[tool-that-cannot-say-its-user]]` was in Further Reading (apex-evolve, 2026-08) but not in `related_articles`; added. Cosmetic.
- **Citation ledger** (5 cites): Gendlin 1981 real-correct · Wittgenstein 1921/1922 real-correct metadata, quote-fidelity defect fixed · Nagel 1974 real-correct, stance leg newly run · Levine 1983 real-correct, stance leg newly run · Chalmers 1996 real-correct metadata, orphan fixed. `find_superlative_claims` empty — no currency-sensitive claims. Wittgenstein metadata deliberately left as "Trans. C.K. Ogden (1922)": the 1922 Kegan Paul translation is now generally credited to F.P. Ramsey with Ogden as editor, but "trans. Ogden" is the universal convention and changing it would read as an error; recorded in the review rather than altered.
- **Calibration** clean and re-confirmed; no possibility→probability slippage. Five-mode taxonomy preserved untouched per three prior reviews' standing instruction. Tenet anchors `^dualism` / `^bidirectional-interaction` / `^occams-limits` all resolve; all 12 wikilink targets exist.
- **Stability note amended, not inherited.** The prior "expect a no-op" prediction is withdrawn. Metadata is settled; quotations have now been checked against genuine raw sources with a translation-discriminating control; the orphan and stance legs have been run. A future pass may treat those as discharged absent a References change — but must not read "converged" in any prior review as covering a leg that review did not name.
- **Published**: `scripts/sync.py` run, Hugo mirror regenerated.
- **Output**: [deep-review-2026-09-17-phenomenology-of-linguistic-failure](/reviews/deep-review-2026-09-17-phenomenology-of-linguistic-failure/)

- **Lead resolved**: Watkins, *Kant and the Metaphysics of Causality* — **CUP 2005 confirmed** (464pp, ISBN 0521543614, via NDPR/Rauscher); Ch. 1 is literally "Pre-established Harmony versus Physical Influx". ⚠️ Crossref dates the DOI 2004-12-20 (online registration, not imprint) — cite 2005, do not "correct" it. NDPR's "Knutson" is a typo for Knutzen; flagged so it is not propagated.
- **Best find**: Knutzen — the leading influxionist, at the moment influx was carrying the field — conceded in print that "the very specific mode of physical influx … cannot be distinctly understood by us on account of the nature of the thing" (*Systema causarum efficientium* §42, trans. Watkins 2003 p. 10 n. 9). He is the eighteenth-century occupant of the Régis brute-interactionist horn added to [history-of-the-interaction-problem](/topics/history-of-the-interaction-problem/) earlier today, and a better instance because he won.
- **Two premise corrections recorded for the downstream expand-topic**: (1) the task note's "**dominant** academic position" could not be verified in those words at any source I read — the evidence supports a documented *shift*, not asserted dominance; either read Watkins 1998 or weaken the claim. (2) "Wolffian pre-established harmony" is a historiographical simplification both SEP and Lorini actively resist — Wolff held PEH as "a more *probable* hypothesis" about soul-body only and thought "nothing significant turns on" it, and Lorini calls the Knutzen/Reusch influx theory "only allegedly Wolffian".
- **Tenet 5 answer, per the driver's instruction not to assert a clean parsimony-fails moral**: nothing empirical decided it. The simpler, law-preserving hypothesis lost to (a) the Pietist charge that PEH entails fatalism and destroys moral imputability, (b) raw institutional force — the 1723 edict expelling Wolff from Prussia in 48 hours on pain of hanging, and (c) a redefinition, not a refutation: Gottsched recast "influx" as metaphorical so nothing migrates, and Knutzen turned Leibniz's own *vis viva* correction against the conservation objection. The theory that won in 1755 was not the theory that had been refuted in 1700.
- **Flagged unverified, explicitly**: the "Wolff retracted PEH in 1724" claim (search-summary only; **absent** from my verbatim SEP extraction, possibly false); whether Knutzen 1735 and 1745 are one work under two titles (they almost certainly are — the 1745 title contains the 1735 title verbatim — but this rests on a Wikipedia works list and needs a library catalogue); Watkins 1995 *Review of Metaphysics* metadata (Lorini's bibliography only, not Crossref-indexed). No Latin primary was consulted — every Knutzen sentence is at one remove through Watkins or Lorini, and the note says so and instructs the article to attribute it that way.
- ⚠️ **Citation trap recorded**: Watkins 2003's reference list contains **a second, different "Watkins, E. (1998)"** — "Kant's justification of the laws of mechanics", *Stud. Hist. Phil. Sci.* 29: 539-560 — and its footnote 28 cites "Watkins (1998)" without disambiguating. Do not conflate with the *Perspectives on Science* paper.
- **Unreachable this session** (403/Cloudflare on every route): PhilPapers/PhilArchive entirely, the Ergo "Fourth Hypothesis" paper, Dyck's "Power, Harmony, and Freedom", Pitts's "The Mind-Body Problem and Conservation Laws", and a *Kant-Studien* paper on Kant's physical influx. The Pitts one is worth a retry — the Map already cites Pitts on conservation laws.
- **Quote fidelity — all 14 load-bearing quotes grep-verified in raw source with byte offsets printed**, 8 against the locally-extracted Watkins 2003 and Lorini PDFs, 6 against SEP's raw HTML re-fetched independently of the WebFetch summariser (so the SEP passages are not merely a summariser's rendering). ⚠️ **The first flat `grep -F` pass returned three false zeros** — pdftotext line-wrapping and curly quotes/guillemets split the strings. Normalising whitespace, de-hyphenating across line breaks and folding quote characters turned all three into hits. Recording it because two confirming zeros would have looked like evidence that Lorini's quotes were fabricated.
- **One self-caught error**: I initially attributed the SEP "Christian Wolff" entry to Hettche & Dyck from background knowledge. I demoted it to an explicit "byline not captured" flag rather than assert it, then verified it properly from the page's `citation_author` metadata and copyright line — it was right, but it was unverified when written. The check also surfaced the note's best onward lead: **Corey Dyck**, SEP's co-author and its own cited authority on Wolff and pre-established harmony, wrote the PhilArchive paper on this exact debate that Cloudflare blocked — the likeliest non-paywalled route to settling the dominance question.
- **One stranded quote caught during the trim**: compressing the verdict removed Lorini's "valid hypothesis only concerning the soul-body relationship" from the file entirely; a post-trim grep for each quote I had moved caught the zero and it was restored to the Lorini source entry.

- **NEW finding — the recorded wake-up trigger cannot fire autonomously.** Prior coalesce entries (09-14, 09-15, 09-16 ×2, 09-17 09:54) all close with the same prediction: expect the same answer "until a `/condense` or `/archive` commit lands in a content section". - **Sources consulted**: 13 cited (3 read in full and grep-verified: Popper's 1980 Tanner Lecture "Three Worlds"; "Of Clouds and Clocks" as reprinted in *Objective Knowledge* ch. 6; John Gray's 1981 *Reason Papers* review of *The Self and Its Brain*). 2 Crossref-verified DOIs (both Dialectica papers, every field printed). 7 further reviews listed as search-summary-only and explicitly marked not-to-cite.
- **Assess-first verdict**: PROCEED, target section `concepts` (re-measured with `tools.evolution.state.count_section_files`: concepts 326 against a cap of 360 — 34 slots; CLAUDE.md's table still reads a 320 cap and is stale). Driver-measured coverage: Popper in 22 content files with no dedicated treatment; "three-world"/"World 3" in exactly one (`topics/sherrington-dualist-lineage`); "clouds and clocks" and "plastic control" **zero** hits corpus-wide; Feyerabend zero hits in content; "metaphysical research programme" raised twice by outer reviewers and never landed in an article.
- **Load-bearing findings**: (1) Popper explicitly refuses substance dualism in his own voice — "I do not of course recommend talking of two kinds of interacting substances … two kinds of interacting *states* (or events)" (*Objective Knowledge* p. 252, verified); (2) Gray 1981 shows Popper's argument for World 2 runs through World 3, so the Map cannot inherit the inference while declining the third realm; (3) Popper rejected the amplified-quantum-jump model of decision as modelling a penny-toss rather than a rational decision (p. 228, verified) — the Map's own mechanism family, attacked by an interactionist, and absent from the corpus; (4) Popper gave the Map's conservation-objection reply in 1966 (note 46, p. 234, verified); (5) Popper stipulated Occam's razor applies only after irreducibility is settled — a Tenet 5 precedent arriving with Feyerabend's and Gray's objections attached.
- **Corpus defect found**: `topics/sherrington-dualist-lineage.md` L63 dates Eccles's "trialist" self-description to *The Self and Its Brain* (1977); it was already in print in *The Understanding of the Brain* (McGraw-Hill, 1973, ch. 6, p. 189). No argument depends on the date, but the sentence reads as if the collaboration produced the conversion. Not fixed in this pass (reports-only skill; the note records the minimal repair).
- **Marked unverified, per driver instruction**: *The Self and Its Brain* itself (Internet Archive lending; Google Books returns `totalItems: null`, so SearchWithinVolume2 unavailable) — everything about its three parts is Gray's relay; the trialist quote (two secondary carriers, no publisher of record); the claim that Popper called psychophysical interactionism a research programme (**grep-negative** in the full Tanner Lecture — the real, adjacent concept is his "metaphysical research programme" in the 1982 *Schism in Physics* epilogue, chapter title verified, epilogue unread); and Dennett 1979, Cohen 1979/1980, Puccetti 1978, Stack 1979, Vesey 1979 and Crick 1994, all search-summary attributions with phrases that must not be quoted.
- **Citation correction**: the Tanner Lecture is **published 1980** (*The Tanner Lectures on Human Values* vol. 1, Utah, 141–167), **delivered 7 April 1978**. The driver brief and several citing sources give it as a 1978 publication. Running page numbers in the PDF confirm the range.
- **Quote hygiene**: 60 quoted strings checked by whitespace-collapsed NFKC substring match with printed offsets; 56 exact, 4 misses all diagnosed as curly-apostrophe or line-break-hyphen variants (none substantive). Every page attribution was fixed by locating the quote between two surviving running-head page numbers, which corrected four of my own first-pass cites (p. 229→228 ×3, p. 235→234, p. 252→251) and downgraded two Gray cites to the 121–124 span because the p. 124 running head did not survive extraction.
- **Sync**: `scripts/sync.py` run; note present at `hugo/content/research/`; all 11 frontmatter wikilink targets resolve and no body wikilink was stripped.

Two-factor (Davies, Coltheart, Langdon & Breen 2001) confirmed; Davies & Coltheart 2022 abandon the affective-flatness seed for a depersonalisation seed; Gerrans 2022 denies the DP continuum (interoceptive dyshomeostasis). Charland-Verville 2013 PET: metadata verified, no abstract in any index, findings NOT read at the primary; Restrepo-Martinez 2019 pre/post-ECT PET verified (insular hypometabolism persisted, prefrontal normalised). Analytic verdict: Cotard passes the register's "reported from somewhere" test but is neutral between subject-receiving-a-corrupted-report and self-model-misrepresenting-itself; tightens the architecture tier only.

- **Topic**: Change-point signatures in the psychophysical transition law at subject onset, cessation and fission (P3, research-harvest from outer-review-2026-09-14-chatgpt-5-6-sol-pro §4.2)
- **Output**: [change-points-in-the-psychophysical-transition-law-2026-09-15](/research/change-points-in-the-psychophysical-transition-law-2026-09-15/)
- **Sources consulted**: 36 (33 DOIs resolved at Crossref in one batch; 22 abstracts read raw from OpenAlex, Semantic Scholar or the author PDF; Bostrom 2006 quoted verbatim from the PDF; Pinto 2017 volume/pages from Europe PMC; Zhang & Spirtes 2008 and Steyn-Ross 2004 cited for metadata only and flagged)
- **Assess-first verdict**: do not decline. `concepts/direction-of-interface-change` catalogues direction-dependent signatures of one subject's interface and carries nothing on census arity; 0 live/archived articles contain `change-point`; Vetoed bank 0 hits; concepts gate 326/360 at write time.
- **Key findings**: (1) the Map's own [P-SC3](/positions/subject-census/#p-sc3)/[P-CS4](/positions/consciousness-scope/#p-cs4) remove anaesthesia, DoC recovery and sleep from the reviewer's six domains — same subject both sides — which converts the entire neural-inertia/criticality literature from evidence into the control a census signature must be distinguished from; (2) the reviewer's "theorem" is ill-posed in conditioning-grain terms across an arity change and well-posed in causal-abstraction terms (Rubenstein 2017 exact transformation; Beckers & Halpern 2019 uniform transformation exposes the balancing-law loophole of horn (c)); (3) under causal minimality, horn (b) does not leave the census "real but idle" — it deletes the variable, so [P-SC1](/positions/subject-census/#p-sc1) and horn (b) are jointly unstable (note's own derivation, flagged for source-check); (4) Bostrom 2006's fractional-experience result is the sharpest rival — a non-integer census makes change-point the wrong formalism — and the Map's haecceity reading is what licenses the discrete prediction; (5) the three fission readings (none / at-most-one / Parfit) predict 3 / 1 / 0 arity events at one moment, giving today's [P-I4](/positions/individuation-and-subjecthood/#p-i4) conditional verdict an in-principle empirical shadow though unreadable by [P-I3](/positions/individuation-and-subjecthood/#p-i3).
- **Positioned against**: [P-SC1](/positions/subject-census/#p-sc1) (trilemma inherited, time index added), [P-SC2](/positions/subject-census/#p-sc2) (cannot discharge; can date third-party evidence), [P-SC3](/positions/subject-census/#p-sc3) (the splitting entry), [P-I4](/positions/individuation-and-subjecthood/#p-i4) Updated 2026-09-16 (pairing-under-division as change-point count).
- **Recommended next**: expand-topic → `concepts/census-change-points` (≤3,000 words); then a `positions-evolve` task for [P-SC1](/positions/subject-census/#p-sc1) (minimality instability) and [P-SC3](/positions/subject-census/#p-sc3) (new shift trigger). Not minted here.
- **Gaps flagged**: no primary source for the disconnection-syndrome resolution timeline; Casarotto 0.31 cut-off only in secondary sources; no literature applies change-point detection to consciousness-conditioned outcome structure.

## 2026-09-15T08:30:00+00:00 - research-voids
- **Status**: Success
- **Topic**: The Categorical Perception Void — consciousness receives continua pre-carved (speech, colour, faces, musical intervals, lab-learned textures); the within-category interior is compressed, the provenance of each joint (world-given vs installed) is unreadable from inside, and the continuum survives sub-personally (Toscano et al. 2010: N1 tracks VOT linearly, independent of reported category) but is never the default deliverable
- **Category**: Mixed — Occluded (active compression) with an Unexplorable face that deflates under measurement
- **Output**: [voids-categorical-perception-void-2026-09-15](/research/voids-categorical-perception-void-2026-09-15/)
- **Key finding**: The strong textbook claim that listeners *cannot hear* within-category differences is rejected by the speech field itself as a task artefact (McMurray 2022 JASA; Gerrits & Schouten 2004: no CP in 4IAX, full CP in 2IFC on the same vowels), while the weak claim — warping plus a category-first default readout — replicates across five domains; the surviving void is one of default access and provenance, not capacity. Novel catalogue conjunction: vagueness-void (concepts fuzzy at edges) and CP (percepts sharp where the world is continuous) are complementary edge-representation failures. AI asymmetry: Bonnasse-Gahot & Nadal 2022 (CP auto-induced by category learning in ANNs, auditable by RSA) and Cacioli 2026 preprint ("structural CP": LLM geometry warps at digit-count boundaries the model cannot report — flagged unreviewed). Whorfian colour face carried as real-but-modest (Witzel & Gegenfurtner 2011: not lateralised, n=230; Martinovic et al. 2020: no sinij/goluboj advantage, boundary drifts with context). Voids at 103/115 (gate function); no coverage overlap found (0 voids articles mention categorical perception; the 2026-02-15 categorial-void note is Kantian categories of thought). Added to task_chains.pending_articles with target_section: voids.

## 19:26 - coalesce
- **Status**: Abandoned (reasoned decline — no merge; the steady-state SUCCESS outcome per the coalesce candidate-pool memory)
- **Cap state** (live, `tools.evolution.state.count_section_files`): topics 324/360 (90.0%), concepts 323/360 (89.7%), voids 99/115 (86.1%), positions 17/80. Unchanged since this morning's run; no cap pressure, so pressure argues *against* marginal merges.
- **Pool movement since the 08:56 run today: ZERO** (`git log --since=2026-09-04T08:56:29 --diff-filter=A` over the four content sections returns nothing). A same-method re-run would therefore be pure re-litigation, so this run searched from a fourth independent direction.
- **METHOD — LINK-GRAPH CO-CITATION, never previously run.** The three prior orders all measure the *pair itself*: TF-IDF content similarity (08-24 01:07), length feasibility (08-24 18:07), shared `coalesced_from` ancestry (closed 09-01), plus verbatim shingles (09-04 08:56). This run instead measures **how the rest of the corpus uses the pair** — build the wikilink graph over all of `obsidian/` (workflow and templates excluded), then for every co-cited pair score (a) **inbound Jaccard**, |in(A)∩in(B)| / |in(A)∪in(B)|, and (b) **inbound containment**, max(|A∩B|/|A|, |A∩B|/|B|), which finds a satellite the corpus never reaches independently of its parent. 788 articles indexed, **zero colliding slugs**; 78,057 co-cited pairs, 70,747 scored after requiring ≥4 inbound each and excluding apex (apex↔source overlap is true by construction — the calibration note from the 08:56 run, carried over and applied).
- **⚠️ THE RESULT INVERTS, AND THAT IS THE FINDING — co-citation measures FACTORISATION, not redundancy.** All five pairs read on the merits are heavily co-cited *because* a hub cites its deliberately-factored parts together, so a high score is evidence the corpus is drawing a distinction, not evidence it is duplicating one. This is the same inversion the verbatim screen reported this morning, reached from an independent direction — two orthogonal metrics now both score the Map's *health* rather than its redundancy.

The trailing clause now carries the positive half of the scoping the tenets page states: the aggregate-statistics routes cannot probe it, "a deviation *conditioned* on intention, task or subject still could" ([P-Q3](/positions/quantum-interface/#mechanism-debt)). - **Fifth pass, and NOT a no-op.** Selected on a cosmetic re-qualification: the only commit since the 2026-07-16 review was `c6351d03bd auto(embed-videos)`, which inserted the YouTube block and bumped `ai_modified`. Four prior passes had converged and the fourth was an explicit no-op. Asking what moved *under* the article rather than what changed *in* it surfaced two critical defects.
- **C1 — the article's single most load-bearing empirical claim was uncited and materially overstated.** The "under general anaesthesia the paralysed limb moves" test was called "the single most informative observation", "clinically documented", "a genuine double dissociation", "the cleanest available demonstration", and "clinically robust" — the last of these underwriting the *strongly-supported* tier assignment. None of it carried a citation and no anaesthesia-and-conversion source was in the References block. Web-verified at publisher of record: the systematic source is **Stone, Hoeritzauer, Brown & Carson (2014)**, *J Psychosom Res* 76(2):165-168, DOI 10.1016/j.jpsychores.2013.10.003 — **propofol therapeutic sedation, not general anaesthesia**, an uncontrolled case series in which **five of eleven** patients "were cured or had major improvement", and whose own conclusion is that "the treatment deserves randomized evaluation" (both quoted verbatim from the Europe PMC abstract). Wrong in kind and wrong in strength. Per the deep-review §2 diagnostic test this is a **calibration error, not bedrock disagreement** — a tenet-accepting reviewer would still flag it, because the claim is about an empirical evidence base — and it is an over-claim running *in the Map's favour*. Fixed: section renamed (no inbound anchor links, grep-verified first), Stone et al. named inline with the 5/11 figure and the randomisation call, "anaesthesia" corrected to "sedation" at all six loci, the demonstration language replaced with "a promising dissociation rather than a demonstrated one", and the tier sentence rewritten to partition support — rule-in signs and group connectivity carry *strongly-supported*, the sedation leg is *realistic possibility* on its own.
- **C2 — stranded dependent.** "…evidence that the interface has dedicated reopening machinery" stated the interface localisation in the evidence register, contradicting the article's own two-tier discount and three upstream re-scopings made *after* the last review and never propagated here: `4ab86ad679` (2026-07-30, anaesthesia L111, "three mechanism classes plus one within-class replication"), `356de8a746` (2026-08-01, active-reboot L53, "rather than the four-target span the agent count invites"), `8efc6199fd` (2026-08-22, anaesthesia L145, "an interpretation laid over a wholly neural finding"). Fixed: machinery described as molecular/neural, span scoped to three classes plus one within-class replication, [cross-mechanism-convergence](/concepts/cross-mechanism-convergence/) linked, interface reading marked as interpretation in the upstream's own words.
- **M1** Hoover's sign 63%/100% was uncited and bare. Verified **McWhirter, Stone, Sandercock & Whiteley (2011)**, *J Psychosom Res* 71(6):384-386, DOI 10.1016/j.jpsychores.2011.09.003 — unblinded suspected-stroke cohort, 337 patients, **8** functional cases, sensitivity 63% (95% CI **24-91**), specificity 100% (95% CI 97-100). Cite, cohort, blinding and both CIs added.
- **M2** The hypnosis half was uncited and its "substantial overlap" is not independent replication. Verified **Halligan, Athwal, Oakley & Frackowiak (2000)**, *The Lancet* 355(9208):986-987 — a **single case**, sharing two authors (Halligan, Frackowiak) and a paradigm with Marshall et al. (1997), and answered by critical Lancet correspondence (Terao & Collinson 356(9224):162-163). In an article built on convergence-without-shared-mechanism this is exactly the independence failure its own epistemology link warns about. Stated in the body; cite added with the correspondence noted.
- **M3** "unusually convergent … reproducible" presented one side of a mixed literature. The amygdala-SMA connectivity increase *does* have direct independent replication, so the claim was not fabricated — but amygdala *responses* have come out normal or hypoactive in some FMD and functional-seizure cohorts, most studies use healthy-control-only comparisons, and few adjust for near-ubiquitous mood/anxiety comorbidity. Downgraded to "a recurring network signature", with the non-uniformity stated rather than smoothed.
- **M4** The **Brown 2004 orphan reference**, carried and deliberately deferred since 2026-06-05, closed by citing rather than deleting: named inline as the cognitive-psychology precursor to the Edwards et al. (2012) Bayesian account, phrased from the abstract's own words (Europe PMC, DOI 10.1037/0033-2909.130.5.793). Inline <-> References now cross-reference cleanly both ways.
- **Engagement classification** (editor-internal): sophisticated-functionalist / intra-cerebral redescription **Mode Three** (framework-boundary marking, conceded not refuted); epiphenomenalist on bidirectional interaction **Mode Three**. No label leakage; no editor vocabulary in article prose.
- **Why four passes missed C1**: the prior ledgers audited the nine References entries, all of which are real and correct. `find_superlative_claims` returned empty this pass too — the helper keys on superlative *phrases*, while this overstatement lived in evidential-grade words attached to an **uncited** claim. A superlative sweep and a citation ledger between them do not cover uncited load-bearing claims.
- **Verified**: `scripts/sync.py` run after the edits; the five new strings grep to 1 in **both** trees and the four stale strings ("genuine double dissociation", "cleanest available demonstration", "clinically robust", "evidence that the interface has dedicated") grep to **zero in both trees**; `scripts/validate.py` clean; the review file's memory-slug wikilinks converted to backticks before sync so it cannot block a push (only `todo.md`/`changelog.md` strip-warnings remain, which are exempt).

- **Output**: [ontic-structural-realism-galilean-exclusion-2026-09-04](/research/ontic-structural-realism-galilean-exclusion-2026-09-04/)
- **Task**: P2 — harvested 2026-09-04 from `reviews/optimistic-2026-09-03-explanatory-limit-wing.md`, which deferred the subject here rather than minting an article.
- **Sources consulted**: 18 cited; 10 web searches; 8 artefacts retrieved.
- **Headline finding — the rival does not exist in print.** The reviewer framed OSR as "the one live position that agrees with Galileo's exclusion and then denies there was ever anything excluded". Philosophically right, bibliographically wrong. **Raw-artefact keyword census** (curl + Python grep of the fetched HTML, not a summariser): SEP *Structural Realism* — written by Ladyman himself, rev. 2023-05-18, ~170,502 chars — contains **0** occurrences of "consciousness", "conscious" and "experience", and **1** of "qualia", that one being a citation of Unger (1979) for the view that *qualia are the non-structural components of reality*. Mirror-image: SEP *Russellian Monism* has **96** "consciousness" and **0** each of "French", "Esfeld", "relations without relata". The two literatures are effectively disjoint. The composite rival must be assembled from OSR-about-physics (Ladyman & Ross) plus structuralism-about-qualia (Loorits 2014; Fink/Kob/Lyre 2021; Lyre 2022) — and **both phenomenal structuralists decline the ontic reading**: Lyre, verbatim, "One might be inclined to ask whether neurophenomenal structuralism is a version of structural realism. The short answer is: no"; Loorits classes OSR as a "relatively small minority" radicalism he does not share. So an article would have to *build* its opponent, and must say so on the page.
- **Second finding — the Map already conceded this defeater twice, in one clause each.** `concepts/physical-completeness` says "The Map sides with the epistemic camp" (a declaration, no argument) while giving Maudlin — the opponent on the *other* flank — a full paragraph. `concepts/intrinsic-nature` lists structural realism dissolving quiddities as defeater **#1** and discharges it with "remains contested". Chalmers is more generous to that rival than the Map is, calling these views "respectable".
- **Third finding — it bites the route, not the thesis.** Eliminative OSR would establish that *physical* entities lack intrinsic natures, not that *experiences* do. It removes `physical-completeness`'s ontological-remainder argument; it does not touch Dualism. An article aiming it at Dualism will have mis-aimed.
- **⚠️ Correction to the record.** SEP *Russellian Monism* quotes Chalmers 2013 and appends "(e.g., Ladyman and Ross...)". Raw grep of the Amherst Lecture PDF (pdftotext of the publisher's own file): **zero** occurrences of "Ladyman" anywhere in it. The parenthesis is Alter and Pereboom's gloss, not Chalmers's citation, and must be attributed accordingly.
- **⚠️ Quote-fidelity catch.** Two summariser-supplied quotations were re-checked against raw artefacts. One failed for real: Lyre's "Every phenomenal character is exhaustively individuated by its relations…" is Lyre **quoting his own earlier co-authored paper** (Fink/Kob/Lyre, cited in-text as "FKL, p. 3") and immediately hedging — "We are sympathetic to this reading, but focus here on a methodological point". The summariser presented it as Lyre's flat assertion, attribution and hedge both stripped. The other was a false miss caused by my own case-sensitive grep (`if` vs `If`) and resolved to a genuine hit. Both are recorded in the note's gaps section.
- **Recommendation**: standalone `concepts/ontic-structural-realism` (or `topics/the-steelman-for-ontic-structural-realism`), scoped as the dialectic rather than a survey — with the fold alternative (two edits to `galilean-exclusion` and `physical-completeness`) written out as a genuine option rather than a strawman. Caps **measured this dispatch** via `tools.evolution.state.count_section_files`, not quoted from the stale CLAUDE.md table: topics 324/360, concepts 323/360, voids 99/115, positions 17/80. Real headroom.
- **Word count**: 5294, over the `research/` critical threshold of 5000 (thresholds printed from `tools/curate/length.py:37`, not recalled). Trimmed from 5898 (−10%) by removing redundancy only. The residue is verification apparatus — verbatim quotes, per-source retrieval status, and the gap ledger — which the dispatch explicitly required ("the downstream expand must be able to see what was and was not read"). Cutting further would delete evidence to satisfy a prose threshold. Sibling `envariance-born-rule-derivation-2026-09-02` sits at 5629 for the same reason; if this becomes a pattern the `research/` thresholds are the thing to revisit, not the notes.

Two errors: the string "predominantly" appears **nowhere** in the DIT paper (the conditional is Suzuki & Larkum's, from the *Cell* abstract), and it does not condition the result — the decoupling finding is reported flatly; what is conditioned is the *inference from* the result to consciousness. Rewritten to name Suzuki and Larkum and to place the condition on the step to consciousness.
- **(3) CRITICAL — unearned non-arbitrariness, now grounded.** "his siting of the interface was *evidently* not arbitrary or theory-driven" rested only on the later convergence, which is weak evidence since both parties read the same pyramidal-cell anatomy. The Eccles 1990 abstract supplies the checkable version — he took the bundles "as described by Fleischhauer and Peters and their associates." Added with the Map-relevant payoff: DIT's anchoring evidence is mouse work; Penfield's series was human from the start.

- **The defect**: "If experience were merely information, memory could preserve it completely; that it cannot suggests experience involves the haecceity of *undergoing* rather than *representing*." The conditional is invalid — physical information is routinely compressed, partially encoded, corrupted, overwritten or lost, so "merely information" delivers no guarantee of complete preservation, and lossy memory is precisely what an informational account predicts. As written it handed the physicalist a free reply and rested a haecceity argument on a premise carrying no weight.
- **The transplant** (mirrors the two descendants; no third wording invented): the entailment is **retired, not softened**. An informational account promises nothing about preservation; what it owes is an explanation of *which* information goes missing and why the loss is total for phenomenal character while the propositional record of the same episode degrades only partially. The force is relocated to **indexical non-recurrence** — no stored fidelity delivers *numerical recurrence*, because yesterday's experience was undergone from an indexical position that does not come round twice. Vocabulary is now consistent across the chain: `voids/conceptual-metabolism-void` hands *numerical recurrence* to `inaccessible-past`, which hands it here and to the apex. **Argument 4 still does its work** — it remains the fourth of four considerations and still lands "the haecceity claim in temporal form"; no hole where the fallacy was.
